Well, I... I suppose I do like things done a certain way, but just normal things, you know? Like making sure the laundry is folded neatly or the dishes are put away right.

My husband never complains about how I keep the house, so I must be doing it alright. 

I try to just keep things pleasant and normal. Does that make sense, Doctor? I hope I'm not making it sound like I'm... well, too particular about things.

Oh, no, I wouldn't say I'm an anxious person at all. I mean, of course, everyone worries a little bit now and then, right? Like about the bills or... or if the car makes a funny noise. But just normal things.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, no, I wouldn't say that. I mean, sometimes a silly song gets stuck in my head, or I might remember something embarrassing I said years ago, but... but that's just normal, isn't it? Everyone gets those little thoughts sometimes. 

I don't have any... well, any bad thoughts that I can't shake. I try to keep my mind on good things, happy things. I wouldn't want you to think my head is full of... of strange or upsetting ideas that I can't control. I'm very good at just focusing on what I need to do for the day. 

I hope that makes sense, Doctor. I really don't have any trouble with my thoughts like that. Is that the kind of thing you meant?

People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there.     "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, no, definitely not. I really try to get along with everybody, you know? I think most people are generally good and just doing their best. 

I don't have any problems at the grocery store or with the neighbors or anything like that. I always try to be polite and helpful, and I think people usually treat me the same way in return. If someone is a little short with me, I just figure they're having a bad day. I certainly don't think anyone is out to get me or trying to take advantage. I'd hate to be the sort of person who's always suspicious of everyone else's motives. 

I just try to see the best in people. That's a good way to be, right? I hope I'm answering your questions okay. I really am trying to be helpful.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, no, I wouldn't say that. I really don't feel tense or worried for no reason. Like I said before, I try to keep a very positive outlook on things. 

I mean, if there's a reason to be a little stressed—like if I'm running late or something minor like that—sure, I might feel a tiny bit tense, but that's just normal, right? I definitely don't wake up feeling worried or spend my days feeling on edge when everything is fine. I wouldn't want you to think I'm a nervous wreck or anything like that! 

I really try to just take things one day at a time and stay calm. That's the healthy way to handle life, isn't it? I hope I'm explaining myself well enough for you.

Oh, no, I wouldn't say that at all. I really do try to trust people. I think trust is so important, don't you? 

I mean, I'm not naive or anything, I know you have to be sensible, but I certainly don't walk around doubting everyone. I think if you show people trust, they usually show it right back to you. Well... I mean, I don't want to sound like I'm pointing fingers or being difficult, because I really do try to get along with everyone. Family is so important, isn't it? But... well, I suppose there is one thing.

She's trying to push me out of all the decisions. \n\nAnd no matter how folks try to smooth it over and tell me everything is fine, I know what I’ve been seeing. It's been going on for more than a year now, and it just has me so upset. Does that make sense? I just want to make sure I'm answering your question right.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or worried about this family situation most days?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no, I wouldn't say most days. I really try my best to just... stay positive, you know? I don't want to be a bother to my husband or be one of those people who just frets all the time. \n\nI mean, it certainly upsets me when I see what she's doing, but I try to just keep my head down and tend to the house. I wouldn't say I sit around feeling anxious about it all week. I'm not an overly worried person, really, I just like things to be fair. Is that... is that the kind of thing you were asking? I hope I'm explaining it right.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling irritable or easily annoyed because of these family tensions, even when you're trying to stay positive?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, Doctor... I really try my hardest not to be like that. I don't like to snap at people or... or be short with anyone. That's just not how I am. \n\nEven when she does things that just... well, that aren't right, I just keep my mouth shut and go about my day. I'd hate for my husband or anyone else to think I'm a bitter person, or difficult to be around, you know? I really try to be patient. \n\nSo, no, I wouldn't say I get irritable. I hope that's okay... I mean, I'm just trying to be honest with you. Am I giving you the right kind of information?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days recently where you felt down or lost interest in things you usually enjoy, even taking care of the house?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say that. I really try to keep busy and keep my spirits up. I love taking care of my home—it's... well, it's my pride and joy, really. \n\nI make sure everything is tidy and my husband has a nice dinner waiting for him. I wouldn't let myself just sit around feeling sorry for myself or let the house go. I'd feel just terrible if I did that. I mean, everybody has a quiet day now and then, but I certainly haven't lost interest in my day-to-day life. \n\nI just like to keep things nice and pleasant. Is that... does that help answer your question, Doctor? I want to make sure I'm giving you what you need.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like there are things happening that others don’t notice—like messages meant only for you, or people trying to communicate with you in secret ways?\n\nPatient: Oh, my... no. No, I wouldn't say anything like that. That sounds... well, it sounds a bit frightening, doesn't it? \n\nI just... I just go about my normal day. I don't look for secret messages or anything unusual like that. I'm just a regular person, really. I like things to be straightforward and plain. I wouldn't want you to think I have an overactive imagination or... or that I'm making things up in my head. I try very hard to just stay grounded and focus on my chores. \n\nI hope that's the right answer... I mean, I'm just telling you the truth. Did I understand what you were asking properly, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Do you often worry that others are not truly on your side, even when they say they are, or that they might turn against you if you're not careful?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I don't... I don't think like that. I try to see the good in people, I really do. \n\nI mean, I know my husband loves me, and I try to be a good wife so he... so he stays happy with me. I wouldn't want to go around suspecting everyone of being against me. That just seems like a terrible way to live, always looking over your shoulder. I try to be kind to everyone so they'll be kind to me. \n\nI don't have those sorts of worries... I just try to be agreeable. I hope that's okay to say? I just want to make sure I'm being the sort of patient you need me to be.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself needing to do things in a very specific way, even if others might not see the need for it?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I suppose I do like things done a certain way, but just normal things, you know? Like making sure the laundry is folded neatly or the dishes are put away right. \n\nI don't think it's anything... well, anything strange or overly specific. I just like a tidy house. I think any good homemaker would feel the same way, don't you? I wouldn't say I *need* to do it, it's just... it's just how I prefer it. I certainly wouldn't want to come across as fussy or difficult to live with. My husband never complains about how I keep the house, so I must be doing it alright. \n\nI try to just keep things pleasant and normal. Does that make sense, Doctor? I hope I'm not making it sound like I'm... well, too particular about things.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find that you need to do things in a very specific, exact way, even when it might seem unnecessary to others, and does it cause you distress if things aren’t done that way?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... I'm sorry, did I not explain it right just a moment ago? I apologize if I wasn't clear... \n\nBut no, I don't get distressed about things like that. Not at all. If something isn't done exactly right, like... well, if a towel gets folded differently or a cup is put in the wrong cupboard, I just fix it. It doesn't upset me or make me feel anxious or anything. I promise I don't get worked up over little things. \n\nI try very hard to just be easygoing about my day. I wouldn't want anyone to think I'm high-strung or... or difficult to be around, you know? I just take things as they come. \n\nAm I making sense? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the answers you need this time.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling very anxious or worried about a number of different things in your life, not just the family situation, over the past several months?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say I'm an anxious person at all. I mean, of course, everyone worries a little bit now and then, right? Like about the bills or... or if the car makes a funny noise. But just normal things. \n\nI definitely don't sit around worrying all day. I try to keep a positive attitude. I think it's so important to just... count your blessings and not complain too much. My husband always says I'm pretty good at just taking things in stride, and I really try to be. I wouldn't want to be one of those people who is always fretting and bringing everyone else down. \n\nI just try to handle whatever comes up and keep a smile on my face. That's the right way to look at it, don't you think? I hope that's what you were asking.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts that kept coming into your mind that you didn’t want to have, and that you tried hard to ignore or get rid of?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say that. I mean, sometimes a silly song gets stuck in my head, or I might remember something embarrassing I said years ago, but... but that's just normal, isn't it? Everyone gets those little thoughts sometimes. \n\nI don't have any... well, any bad thoughts that I can't shake. I try to keep my mind on good things, happy things. I wouldn't want you to think my head is full of... of strange or upsetting ideas that I can't control. I'm very good at just focusing on what I need to do for the day. \n\nI hope that makes sense, Doctor. I really don't have any trouble with my thoughts like that. Is that the kind of thing you meant?\n\nDoctor: Have you felt, outside of this family situation, that people are treating you unfairly or taking advantage of you in other areas of your life?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, definitely not. I really try to get along with everybody, you know? I think most people are generally good and just doing their best. \n\nI don't have any problems at the grocery store or with the neighbors or anything like that. I always try to be polite and helpful, and I think people usually treat me the same way in return. If someone is a little short with me, I just figure they're having a bad day. I certainly don't think anyone is out to get me or trying to take advantage. I'd hate to be the sort of person who's always suspicious of everyone else's motives. \n\nI just try to see the best in people. That's a good way to be, right? I hope I'm answering your questions okay. I really am trying to be helpful.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling worried or tense most days, even when there isn't anything specific bothering you?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say that. I really don't feel tense or worried for no reason. Like I said before, I try to keep a very positive outlook on things. \n\nI mean, if there's a reason to be a little stressed—like if I'm running late or something minor like that—sure, I might feel a tiny bit tense, but that's just normal, right? I definitely don't wake up feeling worried or spend my days feeling on edge when everything is fine. I wouldn't want you to think I'm a nervous wreck or anything like that! \n\nI really try to just take things one day at a time and stay calm. That's the healthy way to handle life, isn't it? I hope I'm explaining myself well enough for you.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt detached from yourself or as though you're going through life on autopilot, like you're not fully present in the moment?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. I don't feel like that at all. I try very hard to be present and... well, engaged with whatever I'm doing. \n\nI know some people talk about feeling disconnected or like they're just going through the motions, but I really try to appreciate every little moment. Whether I'm just doing the dishes or folding laundry, I like to stay focused on it. I think it's so important to stay grounded, don't you? \n\nI wouldn't want to just drift through my days on autopilot. That doesn't sound very healthy. I always try to be right here, right now, paying attention to my life. I hope that's the right way to look at it. Does that make sense, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you can’t fully trust others, even if you don’t think they’re actively trying to harm you?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say that at all. I really do try to trust people. I think trust is so important, don't you? \n\nI mean, I'm not naive or anything, I know you have to be sensible, but I certainly don't walk around doubting everyone. I think if you show people trust, they usually show it right back to you. I really try to be open and give people the benefit of the doubt. I wouldn't want to be the kind of person who is always second-guessing what people say or do. That just seems like a very unhappy way to live. \n\nI try to just take people as they are and believe they mean well. That's a good approach, right? I'm really trying my best to be a positive person. I hope I'm giving you the kind of answers you need.\n\nDoctor: Have you, personally, ever felt that people were lying to you, betraying you, or trying to trick you, even if you didn’t think they were doing it out of malice?\n\nPatient: Well... I mean, I don't want to sound like I'm pointing fingers or being difficult, because I really do try to get along with everyone. Family is so important, isn't it? But... well, I suppose there is one thing. \n\nI actually came in today because... well, it's my husband's sister. She's been getting very involved in the family business lately. And I know people say she's just trying to help, but... I've been watching her for over a year now, and it's just so clear to me that she's trying to get control of the money and push me out of making any decisions. \n\nI really don't like to think badly of people, and I know the rest of the family tries to smooth it over and say it's just a misunderstanding, but... I know what I'm seeing. I'm not crazy for noticing these things, am I? I just want to protect what's ours, and I feel like I have to be careful around her. I hope it's okay that I'm sharing this with you... I don't want you to think I'm just a paranoid person or a complainer.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

If something isn't done exactly right, like... well, if a towel gets folded differently or a cup is put in the wrong cupboard, I just fix it. It doesn't upset me or make me feel anxious or anything. I promise I don't get worked up over little things. \n\nI try very hard to just be easygoing about my day. I wouldn't want anyone to think I'm high-strung or... or difficult to be around, you know? I just take things as they come. \n\nAm I making sense? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the answers you need this time.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling very anxious or worried about a number of different things in your life, not just the family situation, over the past several months?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say I'm an anxious person at all. I mean, of course, everyone worries a little bit now and then, right? Like about the bills or... or if the car makes a funny noise. But just normal things. \n\nI definitely don't sit around worrying all day. I try to keep a positive attitude. I think it's so important to just... count your blessings and not complain too much. My husband always says I'm pretty good at just taking things in stride, and I really try to be. I wouldn't want to be one of those people who is always fretting and bringing everyone else down. \n\nI just try to handle whatever comes up and keep a smile on my face. That's the right way to look at it, don't you think? I hope that's what you were asking.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts that kept coming into your mind that you didn’t want to have, and that you tried hard to ignore or get rid of?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say that. I mean, sometimes a silly song gets stuck in my head, or I might remember something embarrassing I said years ago, but... but that's just normal, isn't it? Everyone gets those little thoughts sometimes. \n\nI don't have any... well, any bad thoughts that I can't shake. I try to keep my mind on good things, happy things. I wouldn't want you to think my head is full of... of strange or upsetting ideas that I can't control. I'm very good at just focusing on what I need to do for the day. \n\nI hope that makes sense, Doctor. I really don't have any trouble with my thoughts like that. Is that the kind of thing you meant?\n\nDoctor: Have you felt, outside of this family situation, that people are treating you unfairly or taking advantage of you in other areas of your life?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, definitely not. I really try to get along with everybody, you know? I think most people are generally good and just doing their best. \n\nI don't have any problems at the grocery store or with the neighbors or anything like that. I always try to be polite and helpful, and I think people usually treat me the same way in return. If someone is a little short with me, I just figure they're having a bad day. I certainly don't think anyone is out to get me or trying to take advantage. I'd hate to be the sort of person who's always suspicious of everyone else's motives. \n\nI just try to see the best in people. That's a good way to be, right? I hope I'm answering your questions okay. I really am trying to be helpful.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling worried or tense most days, even when there isn't anything specific bothering you?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say that. I really don't feel tense or worried for no reason. Like I said before, I try to keep a very positive outlook on things. \n\nI mean, if there's a reason to be a little stressed—like if I'm running late or something minor like that—sure, I might feel a tiny bit tense, but that's just normal, right? I definitely don't wake up feeling worried or spend my days feeling on edge when everything is fine. I wouldn't want you to think I'm a nervous wreck or anything like that! \n\nI really try to just take things one day at a time and stay calm. That's the healthy way to handle life, isn't it? I don't feel like that at all. I try very hard to be present and... well, engaged with whatever I'm doing. \n\nI know some people talk about feeling disconnected or like they're just going through the motions, but I really try to appreciate every little moment. I think if you show people trust, they usually show it right back to you. I really try to be open and give people the benefit of the doubt. I wouldn't want to be the kind of person who is always second-guessing what people say or do. Oh, no, I don't think so. I mean, I know some people get really stuck on doing things a certain way, like checking the locks a dozen times or washing their hands over and over, but I don't have anything like that.

I just want to make sure I'm being the sort of patient you need me to be.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself needing to do things in a very specific way, even if others might not see the need for it?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I suppose I do like things done a certain way, but just normal things, you know? Like making sure the laundry is folded neatly or the dishes are put away right. \n\nI don't think it's anything... well, anything strange or overly specific. I just like a tidy house. But just normal things. \n\nI definitely don't sit around worrying all day. I try to keep a positive attitude. I think it's so important to just... count your blessings and not complain too much. My husband always says I'm pretty good at just taking things in stride, and I really try to be. I wouldn't want to be one of those people who is always fretting and bringing everyone else down. \n\nI just try to handle whatever comes up and keep a smile on my face. That's the right way to look at it, don't you think? I hope that's what you were asking.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts that kept coming into your mind that you didn’t want to have, and that you tried hard to ignore or get rid of?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say that. Everyone gets those little thoughts sometimes. \n\nI don't have any... well, any bad thoughts that I can't shake. I try to keep my mind on good things, happy things. I wouldn't want you to think my head is full of... of strange or upsetting ideas that I can't control. I think most people are generally good and just doing their best. \n\nI don't have any problems at the grocery store or with the neighbors or anything like that. I always try to be polite and helpful, and I think people usually treat me the same way in return. If someone is a little short with me, I just figure they're having a bad day. I certainly don't think anyone is out to get me or trying to take advantage. Like making sure the laundry is folded neatly or the dishes are put away right. \n\nI don't think it's anything... well, anything strange or overly specific. I just like a tidy house. I think any good homemaker would feel the same way, don't you? I wouldn't say I *need* to do it, it's just... it's just how I prefer it. I certainly wouldn't want to come across as fussy or difficult to live with. My husband never complains about how I keep the house, so I must be doing it alright. \n\nI try to just keep things pleasant and normal. Does that make sense, Doctor? I hope I'm not making it sound like I'm... well, too particular about things.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find that you need to do things in a very specific, exact way, even when it might seem unnecessary to others, and does it cause you distress if things aren’t done that way?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... I'm sorry, did I not explain it right just a moment ago? I apologize if I wasn't clear... \n\nBut no, I don't get distressed about things like that. Not at all. If something isn't done exactly right, like... well, if a towel gets folded differently or a cup is put in the wrong cupboard, I just fix it. It doesn't upset me or make me feel anxious or anything. I promise I don't get worked up over little things. \n\nI try very hard to just be easygoing about my day. I wouldn't want anyone to think I'm high-strung or... or difficult to be around, you know? I just take things as they come. \n\nAm I making sense? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the answers you need this time.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling very anxious or worried about a number of different things in your life, not just the family situation, over the past several months?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say I'm an anxious person at all. I mean, of course, everyone worries a little bit now and then, right? Like about the bills or... or if the car makes a funny noise. But just normal things. \n\nI definitely don't sit around worrying all day. I try to keep a positive attitude. I think it's so important to just... count your blessings and not complain too much. My husband always says I'm pretty good at just taking things in stride, and I really try to be. I wouldn't want to be one of those people who is always fretting and bringing everyone else down. \n\nI just try to handle whatever comes up and keep a smile on my face. That's the right way to look at it, don't you think? I hope that's what you were asking.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts that kept coming into your mind that you didn’t want to have, and that you tried hard to ignore or get rid of?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say that. I mean, sometimes a silly song gets stuck in my head, or I might remember something embarrassing I said years ago, but... but that's just normal, isn't it? Everyone gets those little thoughts sometimes. \n\nI don't have any... well, any bad thoughts that I can't shake. I try to keep my mind on good things, happy things. I wouldn't want you to think my head is full of... of strange or upsetting ideas that I can't control. I'm very good at just focusing on what I need to do for the day. \n\nI hope that makes sense, Doctor. I really don't have any trouble with my thoughts like that. Is that the kind of thing you meant?\n\nDoctor: Have you felt, outside of this family situation, that people are treating you unfairly or taking advantage of you in other areas of your life?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, definitely not. I really try to get along with everybody, you know? I think most people are generally good and just doing their best. \n\nI don't have any problems at the grocery store or with the neighbors or anything like that. I always try to be polite and helpful, and I think people usually treat me the same way in return. If someone is a little short with me, I just figure they're having a bad day. I certainly don't think anyone is out to get me or trying to take advantage. I'd hate to be the sort of person who's always suspicious of everyone else's motives. \n\nI just try to see the best in people. That's a good way to be, right? I hope I'm answering your questions okay. I really am trying to be helpful.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling worried or tense most days, even when there isn't anything specific bothering you?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say that. I really don't feel tense or worried for no reason. Like I said before, I try to keep a very positive outlook on things. \n\nI mean, if there's a reason to be a little stressed—like if I'm running late or something minor like that—sure, I might feel a tiny bit tense, but that's just normal, right? I definitely don't wake up feeling worried or spend my days feeling on edge when everything is fine. I wouldn't want you to think I'm a nervous wreck or anything like that! \n\nI really try to just take things one day at a time and stay calm. That's the healthy way to handle life, isn't it? I hope I'm explaining myself well enough for you.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt detached from yourself or as though you're going through life on autopilot, like you're not fully present in the moment?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. I don't feel like that at all. I try very hard to be present and... well, engaged with whatever I'm doing. \n\nI know some people talk about feeling disconnected or like they're just going through the motions, but I really try to appreciate every little moment. Whether I'm just doing the dishes or folding laundry, I like to stay focused on it. I think it's so important to stay grounded, don't you? \n\nI wouldn't want to just drift through my days on autopilot. That doesn't sound very healthy. I always try to be right here, right now, paying attention to my life. I hope that's the right way to look at it. Does that make sense, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you can’t fully trust others, even if you don’t think they’re actively trying to harm you?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say that at all. I really do try to trust people. I think trust is so important, don't you? \n\nI mean, I'm not naive or anything, I know you have to be sensible, but I certainly don't walk around doubting everyone. I think if you show people trust, they usually show it right back to you. I really try to be open and give people the benefit of the doubt. I wouldn't want to be the kind of person who is always second-guessing what people say or do. That just seems like a very unhappy way to live. \n\nI try to just take people as they are and believe they mean well. That's a good approach, right? I'm really trying my best to be a positive person. I hope I'm giving you the kind of answers you need.\n\nDoctor: Have you, personally, ever felt that people were lying to you, betraying you, or trying to trick you, even if you didn’t think they were doing it out of malice?\n\nPatient: Well... If something doesn't work out the way I thought it would, I just take a deep breath and try to find a different way to look at it. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, no, definitely not! I've always prided myself on being a very trusting and open person. I really try to see the best in everyone I meet. I think most people are good at heart, don't you? 

I've never been the sort to hold grudges or look for hidden meanings when people are just trying to be nice. I've always gotten along wonderfully with my neighbors and folks at church. It's just... it's just this one very specific thing with my sister-in-law. That's why it's been so upsetting to me, because it's so unlike me to feel this way. 

I'd hate for you to think I'm just a suspicious person who goes around looking for trouble. That's not me at all. I hope you can see that I'm generally very agreeable and easy to get along with. Does that make sense? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture of who I am.
